# ITEMS-RT-2-5 — the REFERENT TABLE §§2–5, one row per item, kept for retrospective validation

**Slice.** `RT-2-5` — the REFERENT TABLE's sections 2 (six desks' noun rows + each desk's REFUSED table), 3 (the overlaps OV-1 … OV-39), 4 (29 power slots · 19 visibility rows) and 5 (the wrong-layer findings in the shipped rows), as re-classified on 2026-09-12 under ADDENDUM 14.

**Source packet.** `rewrite/retro/evidence/convert-RT-2-5.md` (the fold's own conversion sheet: id · gist · verdict · ground · new permission).
**Verbatim law.** `rewrite/retro/verbatim/REFERENT-TABLE.as-ratified.md` §§2–5, at `f2da5a3ee`. The `the old law, VERBATIM` column below reproduces each item's source row **cell for cell, with its own column labels**, joined by ` ‖ `; nothing is paraphrased and nothing is dropped.

**Items: 567.** Verdict tally: **CONTRADICTION 193 · MODEL 13 · SCOPE 10 · SPLIT 24 · STRUCK 327.** Both the count and the tally reproduce the packet's own header figures exactly.

**What this slice of the old law was FOR.** Sections 2–5 of the REFERENT TABLE were the instrument of the REFERENT LAW (ADDENDUM 11, ratified as corrected in ADDENDUM 13B): *a word's referent is fixed by the READ'S LAYER, not by the word.* Every noun a desk could print was assigned to a layer — BODY (an institution row), HOLDER (a citable record-keeper), ORGAN-UNDER-POWER, ROLE, AGGREGATE (a band or label computed over bodies and referring to none), NONE — and a face was allowed a noun only where the pool's own read stood at that layer. A "garrison" needed a garrison-bucket read; a "roll" needed the muster HOLDER; an aggregate band read could name no institution at all; a citation had to come from a record the read actually reached. §2 laid the assignments out desk by desk and recorded, in a REFUSED table per desk, every assignment the Fable refuters knocked down. §3 listed the engine words that sit in several registers at once, so a writer could not lean on one register's meaning. §4 fixed which slot renders which power, and which face (player or DM pen line) was licensed to name each standing. §5 was the audit: 189 shipped sentences caught using a word at the wrong layer for the read behind them. The whole apparatus answered one question — *did the record LICENSE this noun?* — and under ADDENDUM 14 that is no longer the test. The test is now **a face is lawful unless it CONTRADICTS the record**; silence is permission, and four floors survive: (1) no self-contradiction against the whole town's record or another surface on the same page; (2) no invented HISTORY, DATE or NUMBER (THE PROMISE); (3) product scope and the deity doctrine; (4) the engine's own positive model. The five fold rules applied to this slice are quoted at the head of the source packet; a reviewer who wants to overturn a family of rows should argue with the rule rather than with 567 cells.

**How to read a row.** `the ground` for a surviving item names the FIELD, file and line that denies a breach; for a STRUCK item it states in plain words what the bar policed *instead of* truth. `what a writer may NOW do` is the permission actually handed back — argue with that column. `VALIDATION` is blank for the later Fable reviewer's mark.

**Discrepancy found.** The packet's own §"THE FIVE RULES THIS FOLD APPLIED" is subtitled *"so the chair can veto the rule, not 527 rows"* — **527**, where the packet's Counts line, the section arithmetic and this file's row-by-row count all give **567**. It is a typo in the subtitle, not a missing forty items: every section's row count is reproduced here and sums to 567. No other discrepancy: each of the 21 source tables aligns one-to-one and in order with its verbatim section (each verbatim section carries exactly one header row plus the same number of body rows), and every id that appears in both files matches.

## THE STRIKES MOST LIKELY TO BE WRONG

Five places where this slice's fold may have struck a bar that was carrying truth after all. The case **against the strike** is stated as strongly as it can be put; a reviewer should start here.

### 1. `RT2.1-14` + `RT2.2-07` — "the court" struck because `hasCourtSystem` is satisfied by the required HALL

**What was struck.** The bar "the court (body) only where a courthouse row resolves; otherwise the function word". The ground: `priorityHelpers.js:55` sets `hasCourtSystem` from `town hall` / `city hall`, both REQUIRED, so nearly every town+ is `hasCourtSystem` through its hall — "the engine's own reading of a court system". A writer may now call it the court, the bench, the town's law at any `hasCourtSystem` town.

**The case against the strike.** `hasCourtSystem` is a *capability* proxy, not an existence claim, and the fold's own floor 1 is about what is printed **beside** the face. DS-DEF-6 prints `Legal Infrastructure: None` on the same desk, and the institutions roster printed on the same page lists the town's rows by name — a town with `Town hall` and no `Courthouse` shows a reader a hall and no court. A face that says "the court sat late" on that town states a body the roster does not carry: exactly the label trap the fold KEPT for walls (`RT2.1-01`), the watch (`RT2.1-13`), the granary (`RT2.1-18`) and the church (`RT2.1-20`). `RT2.1-20` is the decisive comparison: `hasChurch` is likewise TRUE, and the fold nonetheless kept the church as CONTRADICTION because *the town's roster carries no church row*. The court strike and the church keep are two opposite rulings on one structure of evidence, and the difference between them is only which surface the folder happened to look at. If the roster beats the flag for the church, it beats the flag for the court, and `RT2.1-14` / `RT2.2-07` should have been SPLIT — the courthouse existence kept, the function words released.

### 2. `G-F3` · `G-F21` · `G-F23` · `G-F25` · `G-F30` — "the hall" sentences struck as place-metonyms

**What was struck.** Fold rule 2: "Existence claims meet the roster; metonyms do not." So "what the hall issues", "what is argued in the hall now", "neither holds enough of the hall to end the argument", "arrangements the hall would rather not itemise", "Instructions from {settlement}'s hall are being complied with slowly" are all released as figures of speech, while `G-F20` ("{settlement} has a hall and a seat in it") is kept as CONTRADICTION.

**The case against the strike.** The line drawn is grammatical, and readers do not read grammatically. "Instructions from {settlement}'s hall are being complied with slowly" **presupposes** a hall; a presupposition is not weaker than an assertion for a reader — it is stronger, because it is not offered for inspection. DS-GEN-3 and its siblings fire at every tier, thorp included, where no hall row can be seated at all; the roster printed beside the passage lists no hall; the reader is nonetheless told that instructions came from one. Floor 1 forbids self-contradiction against **another surface on the same page**, and this is that case exactly. The fold accepted precisely this reasoning one row earlier (`G-F20`) and then declined it five times running because the offending noun sat in a prepositional phrase. Either the metonym rule needs a tier qualifier — free at town and up, where `Town hall` is REQUIRED; kept below it — or `G-F20` should have been struck alongside the rest.

### 3. `RT2.1-37` — "the line" struck because rule 3 (same word, same referent) "is a construction rule"

**What was struck.** One spelling with two layers: "the line" is the WALL in DS-DEF-2/5 and the MANNED FRONT in DS-DEF-7. The ground: "no reader is misinformed". A writer may now use the line for either.

**The case against the strike.** A construction rule becomes a truth rule the moment two passages land on one page — and the composer puts them there. The owner's own THREAD principle (09-08) is that adjacent sentences connect; a reader given "the line held through the winter" from DEF-5 and "the line is thinner each season" from DEF-7 will build one referent out of two, and on an unwalled town that referent is a wall the town does not have. That is floor 1 in its literal wording: a self-contradiction against another surface on the same page, manufactured by construction rather than by a single false clause. The fold's test asks whether one sentence contradicts the record; it has no instrument for a contradiction that exists only in the join, which is the very thing the composed-prose architecture created. "No reader is misinformed" is an assertion, not a measurement, and nothing in the packet measures it.

### 4. `VIS-05` · `VIS-18` · `VIS-19` (and the dm-only family behind them) — the authored visibility marks struck

**What was struck.** Where a covert FIELD exists the fold kept CONTRADICTION (`VIS-01`, `VIS-04`). Where visibility is an AUTHORED mark with no field behind it — the `dm-only` marks, §0e's editorial test, the caller-side carriers — it struck the rule, on the reasoning that ADDENDUM 14's four floors name no AUDIENCE floor and so "nothing refuses a leak". The packet records this candidly in its own newFindings.

**The case against the strike.** The absence of an audience floor is a gap in the new test, not a licence, and the fold converted the gap into permission by default. The owner's 09-08 ruling — THE SECRET SHOWN AS A SECRET, the DM pen line — is a product commitment of exactly the kind floor 3 exists to carry; these rows should have landed as SCOPE, not STRUCK. The failure mode is asymmetric and unrecoverable in a way the other strikes are not: a wrongly kept bar costs a sentence, while a wrongly struck audience bar prints a birth capture or a covert impairment on the player face of a shipped world, and no engine field will catch it because the packet has just established there is no field to catch it with. A reviewer should treat "no floor names it" as a reason to ask the chair for a fifth floor, not as a finding.

### 5. `RT2.1-19` + `RT2.2-22` — the hospital struck because `hasHospital` catches monastery · healer · friary

**What was struck.** The bar on calling a friary's care an infirmary. The ground: the engine sets `hasHospital` on all four kinds (`priorityHelpers.js:64`), so "the engine's own reading" licenses the word. A writer may now call a friary's care an infirmary.

**The case against the strike.** The permission handed back is wider than the ground supports. Three of the four matching kinds are buildings; the fourth, `Healer (divine, 1st level)`, is a **person**. On a town whose only match is that row, a face that writes "the infirmary" or "somewhere for the sick" asserts a house for the sick where the roster prints one healer — the roster, again, being printed beside the face. The fold's own fold rule 1 preserved the label trap for precisely this case ("a face that ASSERTS a body ... on a pool that can fire on a town whose roster does not carry that row"), and `hospital` is on that rule's own list of body nouns. The correct verdict looks like SPLIT: the friary/monastery half is genuinely the engine's own reading and should go free; the healer-only town keeps the trap. As struck, `RT2.2-22` additionally hands back no stated permission at all (`—` in the packet), so the row's breadth was never examined.

## APPENDIX — NEW BARS AND FINDINGS ARRIVING WITH THE RE-CUT

The conversion did not only re-classify old law. While answering a different question it **discovered** contradictions no old bar had ever stated, and the source packet recorded them in its own closing section — *"NEW FINDINGS — real contradictions this slice's research shows that no bar ever stated … Each is a refuter's finding under floor 1 today, or a wiring row the chair should charter."* None of them was rowed above, so nine rows in this file (`RT2.1-39`, `RT2.2-04`, `RT2.4R-06`, `OV-7`, `OV-26`, `OV-36`, `VIS-12`, `G-F44-48`, and the `RT2.3R-06` ground) pointed at a section the archive did not contain. They point here.

**Read this appendix before the permission columns above.** Several of these findings are **new bars on writers** — they narrow what a writer may do, and they arrive with the re-cut rather than surviving it. Those rows carry a leading **⚠ NEW BAR** in the id cell, and they qualify the `what a writer may NOW do` cells earlier in the file. A finding that narrows nothing is left unmarked.

**Rows `NF-1` … `NF-13` are the packet's thirteen numbered new findings, quoted in its own words.** **`NF-14` and `NF-15` are the packet's two closing GAPS** — the fold's own questions referred to the chair, carried here because one of them (`NF-14`) is a live bar that no verdict token in this slice records, and the other (`NF-15`) is the open question the corrected `VIS-12` · `VIS-18` · `VIS-19` cells above now point to. They are not among the thirteen and are labelled as gaps.

**Verdict tokens here.** `CONTRADICTION` is used where the finding is a floor-1 self-contradiction on a shipped page today; `WIRING` where the packet calls it an instrument or wiring row for the chair to charter; `GAP` for the two questions the fold explicitly refused to close. `VALIDATION` is blank for the later Fable reviewer's mark, as above.
